// render.mjs — deterministic frame renderer (headless Chromium -> ffmpeg), parallel workers.
//   node render.mjs --project projects/my-video                      full video  -> build/video_silent.mp4
//   node render.mjs --project P --from 30 --to 45 --scale 0.5         preview a range
//   node render.mjs --project P --sheet                               one still per shot -> build/sheets/*.png
//   node render.mjs --project P --stills 3.5,12,40                    stills at times -> build/stills
//   node render.mjs --project P --styles dusk,midnight,golden,neon --at 3.5,12,40   style comparison grid
// Options: --workers N  --scale K  --style ID  --debug  --format png|jpeg  --crf 18  --gl auto|gpu|swiftshader  --out FILE
import http from 'node:http';
import fs from 'node:fs';
import os from 'node:os';
import path from 'node:path';
import { spawn } from 'node:child_process';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { chromium } from 'playwright';

const ROOT = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
const args = {}; { const a = process.argv.slice(2); for (let i = 0; i < a.length; i++) { if (a[i].startsWith('--')) { const k = a[i].slice(2); const v = a[i + 1] && !a[i + 1].startsWith('--') ? a[++i] : true; args[k] = v; } } }
if (!args.project) { console.error('usage: node render.mjs --project projects/<name> [options]'); process.exit(1); }
const PROJECT = path.resolve(args.project);
const BUILD = path.join(PROJECT, 'build'); fs.mkdirSync(BUILD, { recursive: true });
const TYPES = { '.html': 'text/html', '.js': 'text/javascript', '.mjs': 'text/javascript', '.json': 'application/json', '.woff2': 'font/woff2', '.png': 'image/png', '.jpg': 'image/jpeg', '.svg': 'image/svg+xml' };

function listScenes(dir, base) { let out = []; if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) return out; for (const f of fs.readdirSync(dir).sort()) { const p = path.join(dir, f); if (fs.statSync(p).isDirectory()) out = out.concat(listScenes(p, `${base}/${f}`)); else if (f.endsWith('.js') && !f.startsWith('_')) out.push(`${base}/${f}`); } return out; }
const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
  const url = decodeURIComponent(req.url.split('?')[0]);
  if (url === '/__manifest') { res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', 'Cache-Control': 'no-store' }); return res.end(JSON.stringify(listScenes(path.join(PROJECT, 'scenes'), '/project/scenes'))); }
  const p = url.startsWith('/project/') ? path.join(PROJECT, url.slice(9)) : path.join(ROOT, url);
  if (!(p.startsWith(ROOT) || p.startsWith(PROJECT)) || !fs.existsSync(p) || fs.statSync(p).isDirectory()) { res.writeHead(404); return res.end(); }
  res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': TYPES[path.extname(p)] || 'application/octet-stream', 'Cache-Control': 'no-store' }); fs.createReadStream(p).pipe(res);
}).listen(0);
const PORT = server.address().port;
const gl = args.gl || (process.platform === 'linux' ? 'swiftshader' : 'auto');
const LAUNCH = { args: ['--ignore-gpu-blocklist', '--enable-unsafe-swiftshader', '--disable-dev-shm-usage', ...(gl === 'swiftshader' ? ['--use-angle=swiftshader'] : []), ...(gl === 'gpu' ? ['--enable-gpu', '--use-angle=default'] : [])] };
const FORMAT = args.format === 'jpeg' ? 'jpeg' : 'png';
const CFG = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(PROJECT, 'config.json'), 'utf8'));
const FPS = CFG.fps || 24;

async function openPlayer(browser, style) {
  const qs = new URLSearchParams(); if (args.scale) qs.set('scale', args.scale); if (style || args.style) qs.set('style', style || args.style); if (args.debug) qs.set('debug', '1');
  const page = await browser.newPage({ viewport: { width: 640, height: 360 } });
  page.on('pageerror', e => console.log('[pageerror]', e.message));
  page.on('console', m => { const t = m.text(); if (/ERROR|missing/i.test(t)) console.log('[page]', t); });
  await page.goto(`http://localhost:${PORT}/player.html?${qs}`);
  await page.waitForFunction('window.READY === true || !!window.BOOT_ERROR', null, { timeout: 120000 });
  const boot = await page.evaluate('window.BOOT_ERROR'); if (boot) throw new Error('Player failed to boot: ' + boot);
  const [w, h] = await page.evaluate('window.OUT'); await page.setViewportSize({ width: w, height: h });
  return page;
}
const shot = (page, f) => page.evaluate(i => window.renderFrame(i), f).then(() => page.screenshot({ type: FORMAT, ...(FORMAT === 'jpeg' ? { quality: 94 } : {}) }));
const fmtT = s => `${Math.floor(s / 60)}m${String(Math.round(s % 60)).padStart(2, '0')}s`;

async function renderVideo() {
  const b0 = await chromium.launch(LAUNCH); const p0 = await openPlayer(b0);
  const total = await p0.evaluate('window.TOTAL_FRAMES'), fps = FPS;
  const missing = (await p0.evaluate('window.SHOT_LIST')).filter(s => !s.ok); if (missing.length) console.log(`WARNING: ${missing.length} shots have no scene file yet: ${missing.slice(0, 12).map(s => s.id).join(', ')}${missing.length > 12 ? ' …' : ''}`);
  await b0.close();
  const f0 = Math.max(0, Math.round((+args.from || 0) * fps)), f1 = Math.min(total, args.to ? Math.round(+args.to * fps) : total);
  const W = Math.max(1, Math.min(+(args.workers || Math.max(1, Math.min(8, Math.floor(os.cpus().length / 2)))), Math.ceil((f1 - f0) / 24)));
  const partsDir = path.join(BUILD, 'parts'); fs.rmSync(partsDir, { recursive: true, force: true }); fs.mkdirSync(partsDir, { recursive: true });
  const chunk = Math.ceil((f1 - f0) / W); let done = 0; const t0 = Date.now(); const errs = new Set();
  console.log(`Rendering frames ${f0}-${f1} (${((f1 - f0) / fps).toFixed(1)} s) with ${W} worker(s)…`);
  const work = async (k) => {
    const a = f0 + k * chunk, b = Math.min(f1, a + chunk); if (a >= b) return null;
    const browser = await chromium.launch(LAUNCH); const page = await openPlayer(browser);
    const out = path.join(partsDir, `part_${String(k).padStart(3, '0')}.mp4`);
    const ff = spawn('ffmpeg', ['-y', '-v', 'error', '-f', 'image2pipe', '-framerate', String(fps), '-i', '-', '-c:v', 'libx264', '-preset', args.preset || 'medium', '-crf', String(args.crf || 18), '-pix_fmt', 'yuv420p', '-r', String(fps), out], { stdio: ['pipe', 'inherit', 'inherit'] });
    for (let f = a; f < b; f++) {
      const img = await shot(page, f); if (!ff.stdin.write(img)) await new Promise(r => ff.stdin.once('drain', r)); done++;
      if (done % 48 === 0) { const el = (Date.now() - t0) / 1000, eta = (el / done) * (f1 - f0 - done); process.stdout.write(`  ${done}/${f1 - f0} frames  ${(done / el).toFixed(2)} fps  elapsed ${fmtT(el)}  eta ${fmtT(eta)}\n`); }
    }
    (await page.evaluate('window.ERRORS')).forEach(e => errs.add(e));
    ff.stdin.end(); await new Promise(r => ff.on('close', r)); await browser.close(); return out;
  };
  const parts = (await Promise.all([...Array(W)].map((_, k) => work(k)))).filter(Boolean);
  const list = path.join(partsDir, 'list.txt'); fs.writeFileSync(list, parts.map(p => `file '${p.replace(/\\/g, '/').replace(/'/g, "'\\''")}'`).join('\n'));
  const out = path.resolve(args.out || path.join(BUILD, args.from || args.to ? 'preview.mp4' : 'video_silent.mp4'));
  await new Promise((res, rej) => spawn('ffmpeg', ['-y', '-v', 'error', '-f', 'concat', '-safe', '0', '-i', list, '-c', 'copy', '-movflags', '+faststart', out], { stdio: 'inherit' }).on('close', c => (c ? rej(new Error('concat failed')) : res())));
  if (errs.size) { console.log(`\n${errs.size} shot error(s):`); [...errs].slice(0, 40).forEach(e => console.log('  ' + e)); }
  console.log(`\nDone: ${out}  (${fmtT((Date.now() - t0) / 1000)})`);
}
// grid of labelled images, laid out by the browser (works on every OS)
async function contactSheet(browser, items, outFile, cols = 4, cellW = 480) {
  const page = await browser.newPage({ viewport: { width: cols * (cellW + 12) + 12, height: 400 } });
  const html = `<html><body style="margin:0;background:#111;font:600 15px sans-serif;color:#eee"><div style="display:grid;grid-template-columns:repeat(${cols},${cellW}px);gap:12px;padding:12px">${items.map(it => `<div><img src="data:image/${FORMAT};base64,${it.img.toString('base64')}" style="width:${cellW}px;display:block;border-radius:4px"><div style="padding:5px 2px">${it.label.replace(/</g, '&lt;')}</div></div>`).join('')}</div></body></html>`;
  await page.setContent(html); await page.screenshot({ path: outFile, fullPage: true }); await page.close();
}
async function renderStills() {
  const browser = await chromium.launch(LAUNCH); const styles = args.styles ? String(args.styles).split(',') : [args.style || null];
  const dir = path.join(BUILD, args.styles ? 'styles' : args.sheet ? 'sheets' : 'stills'); fs.rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }); fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
  const all = [];
  for (const st of styles) {
    const page = await openPlayer(browser, st); const fps = FPS;
    let targets;
    if (args.sheet) targets = (await page.evaluate('window.SHOT_LIST')).map(s => ({ t: s.start + (s.end - s.start) * 0.45, label: `${s.id}  ${s.start.toFixed(1)}s${s.ok ? '' : '  (MISSING)'}` }));
    else targets = String(args.stills || args.at || '1').split(',').map(Number).map(t => ({ t, label: `${t}s` }));
    for (const tg of targets) { const img = await shot(page, Math.round(tg.t * fps)); const name = `${st ? st + '_' : ''}${tg.label.split(' ')[0].replace(/[^\w.]/g, '')}_${tg.t.toFixed(2)}.${FORMAT}`; fs.writeFileSync(path.join(dir, name), img); all.push({ img, label: `${st ? `[${st}] ` : ''}${tg.label}`, st }); }
    (await page.evaluate('window.ERRORS')).forEach(e => console.log('  shot error:', e)); await page.close();
  }
  if (args.styles) { const n = all.length / styles.length; await contactSheet(browser, all, path.join(dir, 'style_grid.png'), n, 420); }
  else { const per = 24; for (let i = 0; i < all.length; i += per) await contactSheet(browser, all.slice(i, i + per), path.join(dir, `sheet_${String(i / per + 1).padStart(2, '0')}.png`), 4, 440); }
  await browser.close(); console.log(`Wrote ${all.length} stills + contact sheet(s) to ${dir}`);
}
try { if (args.sheet || args.stills || args.styles) await renderStills(); else await renderVideo(); }
catch (e) { console.error('RENDER FAILED:', e.message); process.exitCode = 1; }
finally { server.close(); }

FILE: skills/make-2d-video/kit/tools/beats.py

"""tools/beats.py — turn the timeline into a beat sheet + a draft shots.json skeleton.

  python tools/beats.py --project projects/my-video [--target 3.0] [--max 6.5] [--min 1.2] [--lead 0.15]

A beat is a phrase that should get its own picture. Long sentences are split at commas / conjunctions,
very short fragments are merged. Each beat becomes one draft shot (start = first word - lead).
The AI then edits story/shots.json: names scenes, splits action beats into several quick shots,
merges dialogue beats into longer holds, adds transitions, bars, sfx and music cues.
"""
import argparse, os, re, sys
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
from common import load_json, save_json, ts

ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
ap.add_argument("--project", required=True)
ap.add_argument("--target", type=float, default=3.0)
ap.add_argument("--max", type=float, default=6.5)
ap.add_argument("--min", type=float, default=1.2)
ap.add_argument("--lead", type=float, default=0.15)
ap.add_argument("--tail", type=float, default=1.5, help="seconds of picture after the last word")
ap.add_argument("--force", action="store_true", help="overwrite an existing story/shots.json")
a = ap.parse_args()
cfg = load_json(os.path.join(a.project, "config.json"))
words = load_json(os.path.join(a.project, "timeline", "words.json"))["words"]
total = max(cfg.get("duration") or 0, words[-1]["end"]) + 0.0
end_all = max(total, words[-1]["end"] + a.tail)

# 1) split into phrases at strong punctuation, then at commas/conjunctions if too long
phr, cur = [], []
for i, w in enumerate(words):
    cur.append(w)
    nxt = words[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(words) else None
    gap = nxt["start"] - w["end"] if nxt else 9
    strong = re.search(r"[.?!…:;]['\"”’)]*$", w["word"]) or gap > 0.7
    if strong or not nxt:
        phr.append(cur); cur = []
def dur(p): return p[-1]["end"] - p[0]["start"]
out = []
for p in phr:
    if dur(p) <= a.max:
        out.append(p); continue
    # split at commas / conjunctions closest to the middle, recursively
    stack = [p]
    while stack:
        q = stack.pop(0)
        if dur(q) <= a.max or len(q) < 4:
            out.append(q); continue
        cands = [k for k in range(2, len(q) - 1) if re.search(r"[,–—]$", q[k - 1]["word"]) or q[k]["word"].lower() in ("and", "but", "then", "so", "because", "while", "when", "until", "which")]
        mid = q[0]["start"] + dur(q) / 2
        k = min(cands, key=lambda k: abs(q[k]["start"] - mid)) if cands else min(range(2, len(q) - 1), key=lambda k: abs(q[k]["start"] - mid))
        stack[:0] = [q[:k], q[k:]]
# 2) merge too-short fragments into the previous beat
beats = []
for p in out:
    if beats and dur(p) < a.min and dur(beats[-1]) + dur(p) < a.max:
        beats[-1] = beats[-1] + p
    else:
        beats.append(p)
# 3) build beats + draft shots (contiguous, no gaps)
B = []
for i, p in enumerate(beats):
    start = 0.0 if i == 0 else max(0.0, p[0]["start"] - a.lead)
    B.append({"i": i, "start": round(start, 3), "speechStart": p[0]["start"], "speechEnd": p[-1]["end"], "text": " ".join(w["word"] for w in p)})
for i, b in enumerate(B):
    b["end"] = round(B[i + 1]["start"] if i + 1 < len(B) else end_all, 3)
save_json(os.path.join(a.project, "timeline", "beats.json"), B)
shots_path = os.path.join(a.project, "story", "shots.json")
if os.path.exists(shots_path) and not a.force:
    print(f"{shots_path} exists — not overwritten (use --force).")
else:
    shots = [{"id": f"s{i + 1:03d}", "start": b["start"], "end": b["end"], "line": b["text"], "scene": "TODO", "set": None,
              "bars": None, "in": {"type": "cut"}, "sfx": [], "notes": ""} for i, b in enumerate(B)]
    save_json(shots_path, {"title": cfg.get("title"), "duration": round(end_all, 3), "shots": shots, "music": []})
    print(f"wrote draft {shots_path}")
ds = [b["end"] - b["start"] for b in B]
print(f"{len(B)} beats  |  avg {sum(ds) / len(ds):.2f}s  min {min(ds):.2f}s  max {max(ds):.2f}s  |  video length {ts(end_all)}")
for b in B[:12]:
    print(f"  [{ts(b['start'])}-{ts(b['end'])}] {b['text'][:80]}")

"""tools/common.py — shared helpers (ffmpeg decode, json io, time formatting)."""
import json, os, re, subprocess, sys
import numpy as np


def run(cmd):
    r = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)
    if r.returncode != 0:
        sys.exit(f"command failed: {' '.join(cmd)}\n{r.stderr[-2000:]}")
    return r.stdout


def probe_duration(path):
    out = run(["ffprobe", "-v", "error", "-show_entries", "format=duration", "-of", "default=nw=1:nk=1", path])
    return float(out.strip())


def load_audio(path, sr=16000, mono=True):
    """Decode any audio/video file with ffmpeg into float32 numpy (mono: shape [n], stereo: [n, 2])."""
    ch = 1 if mono else 2
    p = subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-v", "error", "-i", path, "-f", "f32le", "-acodec", "pcm_f32le", "-ac", str(ch), "-ar", str(sr), "-"],
                       capture_output=True)
    if p.returncode != 0:
        sys.exit(f"ffmpeg could not decode {path}: {p.stderr.decode()[-800:]}")
    a = np.frombuffer(p.stdout, dtype=np.float32).copy()
    return a if mono else a.reshape(-1, 2)


def save_json(path, obj):
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path) or ".", exist_ok=True)
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(obj, f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1)


def load_json(path):
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)


def ts(sec):
    m, s = divmod(max(0.0, sec), 60)
    return f"{int(m):02d}:{s:05.2f}"


def norm_word(w):
    return re.sub(r"[^\w']+", "", w.lower())


def sentences_from_words(words, max_gap=0.8):
    """Group word dicts into sentences on . ? ! and long pauses."""
    out, cur = [], []
    for i, w in enumerate(words):
        cur.append(w)
        nxt = words[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(words) else None
        end_punct = re.search(r"[.?!…]['\"”’)]*$", w["word"].strip())
        gap = (nxt["start"] - w["end"]) if nxt else 9
        if end_punct or gap > max_gap or not nxt:
            out.append({"i": len(out), "start": round(cur[0]["start"], 3), "end": round(cur[-1]["end"], 3),
                        "text": " ".join(x["word"].strip() for x in cur).strip()})
            cur = []
    return out


def write_timeline(outdir, words, sentences, source):
    save_json(os.path.join(outdir, "words.json"), {"source": source, "words": words})
    save_json(os.path.join(outdir, "sentences.json"), sentences)
    with open(os.path.join(outdir, "transcript.txt"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for s in sentences:
            f.write(f"[{ts(s['start'])} -> {ts(s['end'])}] {s['text']}\n")
    print(f"timeline: {len(words)} words, {len(sentences)} sentences -> {outdir}")

"""tools/doctor.py — check (and optionally install) everything the studio needs.

  python tools/doctor.py            # report
  python tools/doctor.py --install  # also run the install commands that are safe to automate
"""
import importlib.util, json, os, platform, shutil, subprocess, sys

ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
OS = platform.system()  # Windows | Darwin | Linux
PY = sys.executable
rows, fixes = [], []


def ok(name, good, detail="", fix=None):
    rows.append((("OK " if good else "MISSING ") + name, detail))
    if not good and fix: fixes.append(fix)


def ver(cmd):
    try: return subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=30).stdout.strip().splitlines()[0]
    except Exception: return None


ff = shutil.which("ffmpeg") and shutil.which("ffprobe")
ok("ffmpeg + ffprobe", bool(ff), ver(["ffmpeg", "-version"]) or "",
   {"Windows": "winget install --id Gyan.FFmpeg -e   (then open a NEW terminal)", "Darwin": "brew install ffmpeg"}.get(OS, "sudo apt-get install -y ffmpeg"))
nv = ver(["node", "--version"])
ok("Node.js >= 18", bool(nv) and int(nv.lstrip("v").split(".")[0]) >= 18, nv or "",
   {"Windows": "winget install OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS", "Darwin": "brew install node"}.get(OS, "install Node 20 LTS from https://nodejs.org or: curl -fsSL https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_20.x | sudo -E bash - && sudo apt-get install -y nodejs"))
nm = all(os.path.isdir(os.path.join(ROOT, "node_modules", p)) for p in ["three", "playwright", "@fontsource/fredoka", "@fontsource/nunito", "@fontsource/playfair-display"])
ok("npm packages (three, playwright, fonts)", nm, "", f"cd \"{ROOT}\" && npm install")
chrom = False
try:
    out = subprocess.run(["node", "-e", "import('playwright').then(p=>{console.log(p.chromium.executablePath())})"], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=60).stdout.strip()
    chrom = bool(out) and os.path.exists(out)
except Exception:
    pass
ok("Chromium for Playwright", chrom, "", f"cd \"{ROOT}\" && npx playwright install chromium")
for mod, pipname in [("numpy", "numpy"), ("scipy", "scipy"), ("faster_whisper", "faster-whisper")]:
    ok(f"python: {pipname}", importlib.util.find_spec(mod) is not None, "", f"\"{PY}\" -m pip install {pipname}" + (" --break-system-packages" if OS == "Linux" else ""))
ok(f"python >= 3.9", sys.version_info >= (3, 9), sys.version.split()[0], "install Python 3.10+ from https://python.org")

print("\naiTrends 2D Studio — environment check\n")
for a, b in rows: print(f"  {a:45s} {b}")
gpu = "unknown"
try: gpu = "NVIDIA GPU found" if shutil.which("nvidia-smi") else "no NVIDIA GPU (CPU render / transcription — slower, works fine)"
except Exception: pass
print(f"\n  {OS} {platform.machine()}, {os.cpu_count()} CPU threads, {gpu}")
if fixes:
    print("\nTo fix, run:")
    for f in fixes: print("  " + f)
    if "--install" in sys.argv:
        for f in fixes:
            if f.startswith(("cd ", f"\"{PY}\"")):
                print(f"\n>>> {f}"); subprocess.run(f, shell=True, cwd=ROOT)
        print("\nRe-run: python tools/doctor.py")
else:
    print("\nAll good. Whisper downloads its model (~150-500 MB) the first time tools/transcribe.py runs.")

"""tools/new_project.py — create a project folder for one video.

  python tools/new_project.py --name my-video --voice "C:/path/voiceover.mp3" [--script path] \
      --aspect 16:9|9:16 --resolution 720p|1080p|1440p|2160p [--style dusk] [--fps 24] [--captions]
"""
import argparse, os, shutil, sys
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
from common import probe_duration, save_json

ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
ap.add_argument("--name", required=True)
ap.add_argument("--voice", required=True)
ap.add_argument("--script")
ap.add_argument("--aspect", default="16:9", choices=["16:9", "9:16"])
ap.add_argument("--resolution", default="1080p", choices=["720p", "1080p", "1440p", "2160p", "4k"])
ap.add_argument("--style", default="dusk")
ap.add_argument("--fps", type=int, default=24)
ap.add_argument("--title", default="")
ap.add_argument("--captions", action="store_true", help="burn word-by-word captions (recommended for 9:16)")
a = ap.parse_args()

root = os.path.join("projects", a.name)
for d in ["input", "timeline", "story", "scenes/sets", "scenes/shots", "build", "out", "audio"]:
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(root, d), exist_ok=True)
voice_dst = os.path.join("input", "voiceover" + os.path.splitext(a.voice)[1].lower())
shutil.copy2(a.voice, os.path.join(root, voice_dst))
script_dst = None
if a.script:
    script_dst = os.path.join("input", "script" + os.path.splitext(a.script)[1].lower())
    shutil.copy2(a.script, os.path.join(root, script_dst))
dur = probe_duration(os.path.join(root, voice_dst))
cfg = {"title": a.title or a.name, "aspect": a.aspect, "resolution": a.resolution, "fps": a.fps, "style": a.style,
       "voiceover": voice_dst.replace("\\", "/"), "script": script_dst.replace("\\", "/") if script_dst else None,
       "duration": round(dur, 3), "captions": bool(a.captions), "bars": None, "fadeIn": 0.5, "fadeOut": 0.8,
       "music": {"enabled": True, "gainDb": -17}, "sfx": {"enabled": True, "gainDb": -6}, "loudness": -14}
save_json(os.path.join(root, "config.json"), cfg)
print(f"created {root}  (voiceover {dur:.1f}s, {a.aspect} {a.resolution} @ {a.fps}fps, style {a.style})")

FILE: skills/make-2d-video/kit/tools/parse_script.py
SIZE: 5025 bytes
SHA256: bea28f09cff5fe9d3fbf76274b643280b34724a019a130a1b78f643bbaa92306

"""tools/parse_script.py — import a user's TIMESTAMPED script (SRT, VTT, JSON or TXT).

  python tools/parse_script.py --project projects/my-video --script input/script.srt

Accepted:
  .srt / .vtt          standard subtitle cues
  .json                [{start,end,text}] | {segments:[...]} | {words:[{word,start,end}]}  (seconds or "mm:ss.xx")
  .txt / .md           one line per cue, any of:
                         [00:12.5] text        (00:12) text        00:00:12,500 --> 00:00:15,000 text
                         0:12 - 0:15 text      12.5-15.0: text     [00:12 - 00:15] text
                       lines without a timestamp are appended to the previous cue.
Word timings inside a cue are spread proportionally to word length (good enough for cutting & captions;
run tools/transcribe.py with --script instead if you need exact word timing).
"""
import argparse, json, os, re, sys
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
from common import load_json, probe_duration, sentences_from_words, write_timeline

TS = r"(?:(\d+):)?(\d{1,2}):(\d{2}(?:[.,]\d+)?)|(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)"


def to_sec(m):
    if isinstance(m, (int, float)):
        return float(m)
    s = str(m).strip().replace(",", ".")
    parts = s.split(":")
    v = 0.0
    for p in parts:
        v = v * 60 + float(p)
    return v


def parse_srt_vtt(txt):
    cues = []
    for block in re.split(r"\n\s*\n", txt.replace("\r", "")):
        m = re.search(r"([\d:.,]+)\s*-->\s*([\d:.,]+)", block)
        if not m:
            continue
        body = block[m.end():].strip()
        body = re.sub(r"<[^>]+>", "", body).replace("\n", " ").strip()
        if body:
            cues.append({"start": to_sec(m.group(1)), "end": to_sec(m.group(2)), "text": body})
    return cues


def parse_txt(txt):
    cues = []
    rx = re.compile(r"^\s*[\[(]?\s*(?P<a>(?:\d+:)?\d{1,2}:\d{2}(?:[.,]\d+)?|\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*[\])]?\s*"
                    r"(?:(?:-->|–|-|to)\s*[\[(]?\s*(?P<b>(?:\d+:)?\d{1,2}:\d{2}(?:[.,]\d+)?|\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*[\])]?)?\s*[:\-–]?\s*(?P<t>.*)$")
    for line in txt.replace("\r", "").split("\n"):
        if not line.strip():
            continue
        m = rx.match(line)
        if m and (":" in m.group("a") or m.group("b") or line.strip().startswith(("[", "("))):
            cues.append({"start": to_sec(m.group("a")), "end": to_sec(m.group("b")) if m.group("b") else None, "text": m.group("t").strip()})
        elif cues:
            cues[-1]["text"] += " " + line.strip()
    return cues


def spread_words(cues, total):
    words = []
    for i, c in enumerate(cues):
        end = c.get("end") or (cues[i + 1]["start"] if i + 1 < len(cues) else total)
        toks = c["text"].split()
        if not toks:
            continue
        span = max(0.1, end - c["start"])
        weights = [len(t) + 2 for t in toks]
        acc = c["start"]
        for t, w in zip(toks, weights):
            d = span * w / sum(weights)
            words.append({"word": t, "start": round(acc, 3), "end": round(acc + d * 0.92, 3)})
            acc += d
    return words


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--project", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--script", required=True)
    a = ap.parse_args()
    cfg = load_json(os.path.join(a.project, "config.json"))
    total = probe_duration(os.path.join(a.project, cfg["voiceover"])) if cfg.get("voiceover") else None
    path = a.script
    txt = open(path, encoding="utf-8-sig").read()
    ext = os.path.splitext(path)[1].lower()
    words = None
    if ext in (".srt", ".vtt"):
        cues = parse_srt_vtt(txt)
    elif ext == ".json":
        d = json.loads(txt)
        if isinstance(d, dict) and "words" in d:
            words = [{"word": w.get("word") or w.get("text"), "start": to_sec(w["start"]), "end": to_sec(w["end"])} for w in d["words"]]
            cues = []
        else:
            seg = d.get("segments", d) if isinstance(d, dict) else d
            cues = [{"start": to_sec(s["start"]), "end": to_sec(s["end"]) if s.get("end") is not None else None, "text": s.get("text", "").strip()} for s in seg]
    else:
        cues = parse_txt(txt)
    if words is None:
        if not cues:
            sys.exit("No timestamps found. Use tools/transcribe.py --script <file> to time a plain script from the voiceover.")
        cues.sort(key=lambda c: c["start"])
        words = spread_words(cues, total or (cues[-1]["start"] + 4))
        sentences = [{"i": i, "start": round(c["start"], 3), "end": round(c.get("end") or (cues[i + 1]["start"] if i + 1 < len(cues) else (total or c["start"] + 4)), 3), "text": c["text"]} for i, c in enumerate(cues)]
    else:
        sentences = sentences_from_words(words)
    if total and words[-1]["end"] > total + 1:
        print(f"WARNING: script runs to {words[-1]['end']:.1f}s but the voiceover is {total:.1f}s — check the timestamps match this audio.")
    write_timeline(os.path.join(a.project, "timeline"), words, sentences, f"script:{os.path.basename(path)}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

FILE: skills/make-2d-video/kit/tools/transcribe.py
SIZE: 4205 bytes
SHA256: 0f2bf86f796a8c96cefb06556d99367e3952d79878ca0af247b364d5a097c84b

"""tools/transcribe.py — word-level timestamps for a voiceover with faster-whisper.

  python tools/transcribe.py --project projects/my-video [--model small] [--lang en] [--script input/script.txt]

If a plain-text script is given, its exact wording is kept and aligned onto Whisper's word timings
(so on-screen text and captions use the script's spelling, not Whisper's guesses).
Writes timeline/words.json, timeline/sentences.json, timeline/transcript.txt.
"""
import argparse, difflib, os, re, sys
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
from common import load_audio, load_json, norm_word, sentences_from_words, write_timeline


def align_script(script_text, wwords):
    """Map script tokens onto whisper words with difflib; interpolate the gaps."""
    toks = [t for t in re.findall(r"\S+", script_text)]
    a = [norm_word(t) for t in toks]
    b = [norm_word(w["word"]) for w in wwords]
    sm = difflib.SequenceMatcher(a=a, b=b, autojunk=False)
    times = [None] * len(toks)
    for blk in sm.get_matching_blocks():
        for k in range(blk.size):
            w = wwords[blk.b + k]
            times[blk.a + k] = (w["start"], w["end"])
    # fill unmatched tokens by interpolating between known neighbours
    n = len(toks)
    i = 0
    while i < n:
        if times[i] is not None:
            i += 1
            continue
        j = i
        while j < n and times[j] is None:
            j += 1
        t0 = times[i - 1][1] if i > 0 else (wwords[0]["start"] if wwords else 0.0)
        t1 = times[j][0] if j < n else (wwords[-1]["end"] if wwords else t0 + 0.4 * (j - i))
        span = max(0.05, t1 - t0) / (j - i)
        for k in range(i, j):
            times[k] = (t0 + span * (k - i), t0 + span * (k - i + 1))
        i = j
    matched = sum(b.size for b in sm.get_matching_blocks())
    print(f"script alignment: {matched}/{len(toks)} script words matched to audio ({100 * matched / max(1, len(toks)):.0f}%)")
    return [{"word": t, "start": round(s, 3), "end": round(e, 3)} for t, (s, e) in zip(toks, times)]


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--project", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--audio", help="defaults to config.json voiceover")
    ap.add_argument("--model", default="small", help="tiny|base|small|medium|large-v3 (bigger = slower, more accurate)")
    ap.add_argument("--lang", default=None, help="e.g. en (auto-detect if omitted)")
    ap.add_argument("--script", help="plain-text script to align (keeps exact wording)")
    a = ap.parse_args()
    cfg = load_json(os.path.join(a.project, "config.json"))
    audio_path = a.audio or os.path.join(a.project, cfg["voiceover"])
    from faster_whisper import WhisperModel  # pip install faster-whisper
    try:
        import torch  # noqa
        device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
    except Exception:
        device = "cpu"
    model_name = a.model + (".en" if a.lang == "en" and a.model in ("tiny", "base", "small", "medium") else "")
    print(f"loading whisper '{model_name}' on {device} (first run downloads the model)…")
    model = WhisperModel(model_name, device=device, compute_type="float16" if device == "cuda" else "int8")
    audio = load_audio(audio_path, 16000)
    segs, info = model.transcribe(audio, language=a.lang, word_timestamps=True, vad_filter=True, beam_size=5)
    words = []
    for s in segs:
        for w in s.words or []:
            words.append({"word": w.word.strip(), "start": round(w.start, 3), "end": round(w.end, 3)})
        print(f"  {s.start:7.2f}  {s.text.strip()[:90]}")
    if not words:
        sys.exit("no speech found in the voiceover")
    source = f"whisper:{model_name}"
    script = a.script or (os.path.join(a.project, cfg["script"]) if cfg.get("script") and cfg["script"].endswith((".txt", ".md")) else None)
    if script and os.path.exists(script):
        txt = open(script, encoding="utf-8").read()
        txt = re.sub(r"^\s*#.*$", "", txt, flags=re.M)  # drop markdown headings
        words = align_script(txt, words)
        source += "+script"
    write_timeline(os.path.join(a.project, "timeline"), words, sentences_from_words(words), source)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

"""tools/verify_kit.py — confirm every studio file was written exactly (run right after installing).

  python tools/verify_kit.py
Prints MISMATCH/MISSING for any file that differs from the tested version; copy those files again from the plugin kit folder.
(Line endings and leading/trailing blank lines are ignored. Files you customise later, e.g. engine/styles.js, will of course differ.)
"""
import hashlib, os, sys
ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
EXPECTED = {
    "audio/dsp.py": "589d305d94d8acc3",
    "audio/mix.py": "a48273c523b68c3b",
    "audio/music.py": "2fb3ce1df2fe5c7d",
    "audio/sfx.py": "2fa56141e2423718",
    "engine/core.js": "cb84a01d65e347a9",
    "engine/fx2d.js": "d09f93f6d44366fa",
    "engine/player.js": "2efc90d6868b1472",
    "engine/rig2d.js": "ec2a665e0aa8ffed",
    "engine/styles.js": "66b00b1b5f87326f",
    "engine/three-kit.js": "02e249e302b95420",
    "engine/ui2d.js": "babd2978a5d2a6db",
    "examples/reaction-time/config.json": "9ee341c65b3cfe9a",
    "examples/reaction-time/scenes/cast.js": "e4faad3c508a5cf5",
    "examples/reaction-time/scenes/sets/cafe.js": "fb9adaa553e87b74",
    "examples/reaction-time/scenes/sets/lab.js": "a2da3ca0fff2d8c4",
    "examples/reaction-time/scenes/shots/01_cafe.js": "931e9c360ba052b3",
    "examples/reaction-time/scenes/shots/02_orbit3d.js": "77897bcae80a501e",
    "examples/reaction-time/scenes/shots/03_explain.js": "c22f0e7a8f505c78",
    "examples/reaction-time/story/bible.md": "e22b689a54461b41",
    "examples/reaction-time/story/shots.json": "9b1921e8e42c2498",
    "package.json": "1bd334931c9fa9de",
    "player.html": "5ffdd7da4a48cd4b",
    "render.mjs": "ba9779c796616616",
    "tools/beats.py": "eb42a8aef4d87584",
    "tools/common.py": "e237321f2f12416e",
    "tools/doctor.py": "ce17e8e1bfcb9b86",
    "tools/new_project.py": "940758644aa95289",
    "tools/parse_script.py": "5163cf3162b2579b",
    "tools/transcribe.py": "ecd48d05a6655d13",
}
bad = 0
for rel, want in EXPECTED.items():
    p = os.path.join(ROOT, *rel.split("/"))
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        print(f"MISSING   {rel}"); bad += 1; continue
    got = hashlib.sha256(open(p, "rb").read().replace(b"\r\n", b"\n").strip()).hexdigest()[:16]
    if got != want:
        print(f"MISMATCH  {rel}"); bad += 1
print(f"{len(EXPECTED) - bad}/{len(EXPECTED)} files verified." + ("" if not bad else "  Copy the files listed above again from the plugin's kit folder, then run this again."))
sys.exit(1 if bad else 0)

# Engine reference

## Folder layout
```
aitrends-2d-studio/
  render.mjs  player.html  package.json
  engine/   core.js styles.js rig2d.js fx2d.js ui2d.js three-kit.js player.js
  tools/    doctor.py verify_kit.py new_project.py transcribe.py parse_script.py beats.py common.py
  audio/    dsp.py sfx.py music.py mix.py
  examples/reaction-time/   (reference project: scenes + story)
  projects/<slug>/
     config.json   input/ (voiceover, script, music)   timeline/ (words, sentences, beats)
     story/shots.json  story/bible.md   scenes/cast.js  scenes/sets/*.js  scenes/shots/*.js
     build/ (sheets, stills, styles, parts, music.wav, mix.wav, video_silent.mp4)   out/<title>.mp4
```
Every `.js` under `scenes/` is loaded automatically (files starting with `_` are skipped). Import the engine with absolute paths (`'/engine/core.js'`) and project files relatively (`'../cast.js'`).

## config.json
`{ title, aspect: "16:9"|"9:16", resolution: "720p"|"1080p"|"1440p"|"2160p", fps: 24, style: "dusk", voiceover, script, duration, captions: bool, captionStyle?: {y,size,max}, bars: null|"wide"|"scope"|"none"|number, fadeIn: 0.5, fadeOut: 0.8, music: {enabled, gainDb, file?, seed?, key?}, sfx: {enabled, gainDb}, loudness: -14 }`

## story/shots.json
```json
{ "title": "…", "duration": 312.4,
  "shots": [ { "id": "s001", "start": 0, "end": 3.4, "line": "spoken words", "scene": "what we see", "set": "cafe",
               "bars": null, "in": {"type": "cut|fade|dip|dipWhite|flash|whip|zoom", "dur": 0.4, "dir": 1},
               "sfx": [{"at": 0.4, "name": "buzz", "gain": -10}], "amb": {"name": "murmur", "gain": -26},
               "params": {}, "vignette": true, "captions": true, "notes": "" } ],
  "music": [ {"start": 0, "end": 42, "mood": "mystery", "intensity": 0.4} ],
  "sfx": [ {"t": 120.5, "name": "impact", "gain": -6} ] }
```

## Shot modules
```js
import { E, V, registerShot, mood, layer, blurLayer, veil, ease, prog, key, wordTime, talkAmount } from '/engine/core.js';
import { drawFigure, POSES, posesAt, breathe, walkPose, bigFace, headPos } from '/engine/rig2d.js';
import * as FX from '/engine/fx2d.js';  import * as UI from '/engine/ui2d.js';
import { lab } from '../sets/lab.js';  import { MAX, DOC } from '../cast.js';

registerShot('s013', { draw(g, t, info) {            // 2D: g = canvas ctx in logical units, t = seconds into the shot
  const cam = { z: 1 + 0.06 * ease.inOut(t / info.dur), x: -30 };
  const { GY, M } = lab(g, t, cam);
  layer(g, cam, 1, g => drawFigure(g, { x: E.W/2 - 40, y: GY, s: 1.4, facing: -1, outfit: DOC,
        pose: breathe(posesAt(t, [[0, 'stand'], [0.4, 'point']]), t), expr: 'smile', rim: { color: M.rim, dir: [0, -1] } }));
  FX.dust(g, t);
}});
registerShot('s008', { kind: '3d',                   // 3D: init once (T = three-kit), draw per frame, optional 2D overlay
  init(T, info) { const st = T.stage({ mood: 'golden' }); /* build */ return { stage: st }; },
  draw(s, t, info, T) { T.orbit(s.stage.camera, t, { target: [0, 1, 0], radius: 6, height: 2.5, from: -0.8, to: 0.3, dur: info.dur }); },
  overlay(g, t, info, s) { /* 2D graphics on top, logical units */ } });
```
`info = { dur, globalT, p, line, shot, ...shot.params }`. An exception inside a shot is printed to the console and drawn as red text on the frame, so check the render output.

## Core, rig, FX and UI API (logical units: 16:9 → 1920×1080, 9:16 → 1080×1920)
- **core.js:** `E` (W, H, PX, fps, vertical, cfg, words, shots), `V(h, v)`, `clamp lerp prog ease{linear,in,out,inOut,sine,outBack,outElastic} key(t, [[t,v],…], ease) mixVal rng(seed) noise(x, seed) shake(t, amp, freq, seed) talkAmount(globalT, from, to) wordTime(word, afterGlobalT)`, colour `rgb css mix alpha shade`, `mood(name) S() setStyle`, draw `rr seg poly circle ring linGrad radGrad glow fillRect backdrop veil text measure FONTS`, camera `layer(g, cam, depth, fn) blurLayer(g, amount, fn, cam?, depth?)`, `registerShot getShot`.
- **rig2d.js:** `drawFigure(g, {x, y, s, facing, pose, rot, outfit, expr, talk, blink, look:[x,y], rim:{color, dir, w}, dim, alpha, prop, shadow, trail})`. Poses: `POSES.{stand, relaxed, talk, explain, point, pointUp, wave, cheer, handsOnHead, crossArms, think, shrug, holdFront, phone, reach, sad, facepalm, ready, crouch, sit, sitBack, kneel, lie(+rot), dive(+rot), kick, jump, fall}`, plus `posesAt, blendPose, breathe, walkPose(phase, {run})`, `face(g, x, y, r, {expr, talk, blink, look, front})`, `bigFace(g, cx, cy, r, {outfit, expr, talk, blink, look, rimColor, rimSide, tilt})`, `headPos(o) → [x, y, r]`, `soccerBall(g, x, y, r, rot)`. Props: `phone, ball, paper, cup, paddle, pointer, tablet, flashlight`. Outfit: `top: hoodie|labcoat|trench|jersey|tshirt|suit|dress|tracksuit|none, color, color2, legs, head: cap|capBack|beanie|fedora|headband|visor|hood|ponytail|bun|curly|spiky|none, headColor, face: glasses|sunglasses, gloves, number, kid`. Custom poses are plain objects with the 11 points; edit a copy (`{...POSES.sit, hF:[34,-188]}`).
- **fx2d.js:** `rain drops dust snow stars fog sun moon godRays lightCone pool lensStreak sparkle lightning speedLines rings skyline hills clouds tree pine lampPost windowFrame facade door chair stool counter screen bed clock floorLines crowd`.
- **ui2d.js:** `titleCard bigLine badge readout callout dimension dataCard compareBars meter blueprint bpStroke lineFigure bubble endScreen captions`.

## three-kit.js (passed as `T` to `init`/`draw`)
`T.stage({mood, fov, fog:[near, far], sky, keyPos, keyIntensity, ambient, bloom})` → `{scene, camera, composer, sun, add(...objs), render(), dispose()}`. Builders: `box cyl cone sphere plane ground(w, d, color, tex) planks tiles windowPane(w, h, {glowColor, cols, rows, power, interior}) shaft(w, len, color, opacity) building(w, h, d, {color, win, lit}) tree3D pine3D crowd3D({rows, perRow, width, step, rise, colors}) figure3D(outfit, {rim, pose, s}) → .setPose(pose)`, `mat basic canvasTex`, and cameras `orbit(camera, t, {target, radius, height, from, to, dur})` and `dolly(camera, t, [[t, {pos, look, fov}], …])`. Units are metres, a figure is ~1.7 m and faces +x. 3D shots get the same grade, bars and grain automatically. Keep exposure moody: let windows and lamps be the brightest things, and keep the floor dark.

# Installing the studio (once per machine or workspace)

The studio is a small Node + Python toolkit that ships inside this plugin, in the `kit/` folder of the `make-2d-video` skill. Copy it into a writable workspace folder, because the plugin folder may be read-only and the studio needs `node_modules`, projects and renders next to it.

## 1. Locate the kit
- The kit is `kit/` inside the `make-2d-video` skill's base directory. Stage commands (`new-video`, `video-styles`, `render-video`, `video-sound`) reach it at `../make-2d-video/kit/` from their own base directory.
- If the path is unclear, search for it: `find / -path "*make-2d-video/kit/render.mjs" 2>/dev/null | head -1` (Linux/macOS/Cowork VM), or `Get-ChildItem -Recurse -Filter render.mjs -Path $HOME\.claude | Select-Object -First 1` (PowerShell).

## 2. Choose the workspace and copy
- Use the user's connected project folder if there is one, otherwise the current working directory. Create `aitrends-2d-studio/` there.
- If `aitrends-2d-studio/` already exists with `render.mjs` inside, it's already installed. Skip to step 4 (doctor), and never overwrite the user's `projects/` folder.
- Copy all of `kit/` into it: `cp -r "<kit>/." aitrends-2d-studio/` (bash) or `Copy-Item -Recurse "<kit>\*" aitrends-2d-studio\` (PowerShell).
- Run `python tools/verify_kit.py` inside `aitrends-2d-studio/`. It must report 29/29. If anything is missing or mismatched, copy those files again.

## 3. Install dependencies
Tell the user, in one short list, what is being installed and why: ffmpeg (video/audio encoding), Node.js packages (renderer, Three.js, fonts), a headless Chromium (draws the frames), and numpy, scipy and faster-whisper (sound synthesis and timestamps).

| Need | Windows | macOS | Linux |
|---|---|---|---|
| ffmpeg + ffprobe | `winget install --id Gyan.FFmpeg -e` (then open a new terminal) | `brew install ffmpeg` | `sudo apt-get install -y ffmpeg` |
| Node.js ≥ 18 | `winget install OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS` | `brew install node` | nodesource or distro package |
| Python ≥ 3.9 | python.org installer | `brew install python` | distro package |
| JS packages | `npm install` (inside aitrends-2d-studio) | same | same |
| Headless Chromium | `npx playwright install chromium` | same | same (`npx playwright install-deps chromium` if libraries are missing) |
| Python packages | `python -m pip install numpy scipy faster-whisper` | same | add `--break-system-packages` on managed Pythons |

## 4. Check and self-test
1. `python tools/doctor.py`. Use `python3` where `python` isn't Python 3, and `py -3` on some Windows setups. Repeat until every line is OK. `python tools/doctor.py --install` runs the npm, playwright and pip fixes automatically.
2. Self-test: `node render.mjs --project examples/reaction-time --sheet --scale 0.3`. It should write `examples/reaction-time/build/sheets/sheet_01.png` with 14 finished frames (café scenes, giant faces, a 3D orbit, blueprint charts, a title card) and print no errors. Open the PNG to confirm, then delete `examples/reaction-time/build/`.
3. Whisper downloads its model the first time it runs (~75 MB for `base`, ~500 MB for `small`). That needs internet access to huggingface.co.

## Notes
- On Linux servers and the Cowork VM there's no GPU. Chromium uses SwiftShader automatically (`--gl swiftshader` is the Linux default), so everything works, just slower.
- Disk: ~600 MB for node_modules + Chromium, plus renders (a 10-minute 1080p video is ~150–300 MB).
- Updating: when the plugin is updated, copy the new `kit/` over the existing install. It never touches `projects/`, so the user's videos are safe. Custom styles added to `engine/styles.js` would need re-adding, so check first.

# Rendering, QC and troubleshooting

## Render

**Preview first.** `node render.mjs --project projects/my-video --from 0 --to 30 --scale 0.5` writes `build/preview.mp4`. Watch it (or sample stills). Then send the user a preview or a contact sheet before the long final render.

**Final render:**
```bash
node render.mjs --project projects/my-video [--workers N] [--format jpeg]
python audio/mix.py --project projects/my-video        # builds music + SFX, mixes, muxes -> out/<title>.mp4
```
- Workers default to half the CPU threads (max 8). Each worker is a separate Chromium. Lower the count if RAM is short (about 0.6–1.5 GB per worker at 1080p, more at 4K).
- On machines without a GPU (most Linux servers), Chromium uses SwiftShader (`--gl swiftshader`, the default on Linux). On Windows and macOS desktops the GPU is used (`--gl auto`).
- **Speed guide** (frames = seconds × 24): measured at 720p on a 2-thread cloud CPU, about 2 frames/s, so a 47 s video took about 10 min. 1080p costs ~2× that, 1440p ~3.5×, 4K ~8×. A modern 8-core desktop is ~3–5× faster, and a GPU helps the 3D shots most. Budget accordingly, and for 10-minute videos at 4K plan for a long or overnight render. Long renders should run in the background (`nohup … &` or a separate terminal) while you poll the log.
- `--format jpeg` speeds up capture (~1.5×) with negligible quality loss.
- **Partial re-render after a fix:** re-run the whole video (simple and safe), or render only the changed range with `--from/--to --out build/fix.mp4` and splice it in with ffmpeg if you're comfortable.
- **Final QC:** `ffprobe` duration ≈ the shots.json duration, audio present, then make a contact sheet of the final file with ffmpeg (`-vf fps=1/10,scale=320:-1,tile=6x5`) and look at it.


## QC checklist (run on every contact sheet)
- [ ] No `MISSING SHOT` or red `ERROR` frames, and the console printed no shot errors.
- [ ] Each picture matches its `line`, and the key action lands on the key word.
- [ ] Characters are readable: rim light present, staged against brighter areas, not cut off at the edges, not overlapping text.
- [ ] Outfits and screen direction are consistent within a scene, and every character has a contact shadow or is grounded.
- [ ] Moody value structure (mostly dark, 1–3 bright accents), not flat or washed out, and colours come from the mood/style.
- [ ] Every shot has camera motion and some atmosphere (dust/rain/particles/glows).
- [ ] Text is short, inside the safe area, and legible at phone size. Numbers match the narration.
- [ ] Variety: no more than 3 consecutive shots of the same type or framing, with close-ups and inserts sprinkled in.
- [ ] 9:16: subjects in the middle, nothing important under the captions.
- [ ] Audio: voice clear, music ducked, no SFX pile-ups, final loudness about −14 LUFS.

## Troubleshooting
- **Fonts look like serif defaults** → the `@fontsource` packages are missing. Run `npm install` in aitrends-2d-studio.
- **`Player failed to boot`** → read the message. Usually it's a JSON syntax error in shots.json/config.json or an import path in a scene. Scene import errors are listed at the end of a render.
- **WebGL or 3D shot black** → add `--gl swiftshader`, and check the console for Three.js errors (a misspelt builder or a missing `return {stage}` in init).
- **Out of memory or crashes at 4K** → use `--workers 1` or `2`, and avoid huge blur amounts on 4K (blur cost scales with resolution).
- **Whisper slow** → use `--model base`. **Script match < 90%** → the wrong script or audio. Ask the user.
- **The video is longer or shorter than the audio** → `story.duration` must equal the last shot's `end`. Re-run `beats.py --force` or fix by hand.
- **PyAV/`av` errors from faster-whisper** → the tools decode audio with ffmpeg themselves, so just ensure ffmpeg is on PATH.
- **Windows paths** → quote paths with spaces. Use `python` (or `py -3`) instead of `python3`.

# Sound design

`audio/mix.py` builds the soundtrack from `story/shots.json`. It ducks the music −9 dB and ambience −5 dB under the voice and normalises the master to −14 LUFS (YouTube), or `config.loudness`.

**Music plan** (`"music"` in shots.json): contiguous sections with a mood and intensity (0–1; higher adds pulse, arps and drums). Moods: `mystery, tension, action, wonder, hopeful, sad, triumph, playful, calm, dark, epic`. Change sections at story turns (the title card, a discovery, danger, the climax, the resolution, the science segment), typically every 30–90 s. Audition with `python audio/music.py --mood tension --dur 20 --out test.wav`. To use the user's own track, set `config.music.file = "input/music.mp3"` (it loops or trims and fades the end). Set `config.music.gainDb` (default −17 relative to the voice) for a louder or quieter bed. The generated music is a serviceable ambient/cinematic bed. For premium results, suggest the user's own licensed track.

**SFX cues** (per shot `"sfx": [{"at": 0.3, "name": "whoosh", "gain": -8}]`, where `at` is seconds from the shot start; optional `dur`, `seed`, `pitch`; `name` can also be a path to the user's own WAV/MP3). Library (`python audio/sfx.py --list`, audition all with `--demo build/sfx_demo`):
`whoosh, swish, impact, hit, riser, slowmo, freeze, tick, clock, heartbeat, pop, blip, counter, ding, shimmer, stinger, footstep, kick, whistle, carpass, phonebuzz, phonering, cheer, applause, murmur, rain, thunder, wind, roomtone, city, hum, powerup, glitch, flash, buzz`.
Conventions: `whoosh`/`swish` on camera whips and fast moves. `pop`/`blip` when graphics appear, `counter` under ticking numbers. `riser` 1–2 s before a big reveal, then `impact`/`stinger` on it. `slowmo` going into slow motion, `freeze` on time-stop. `ding`/`shimmer` for realisations and powers. `hit`/`kick`/`footstep` on contact. Gains: subtle UI −12 to −16, normal −6 to −10, big moments −3 to −6. Don't stack more than ~2 cues at the same instant, and leave dialogue clear.

**Ambience beds** (per shot `"amb": {"name": "rain", "gain": -20}`; looped with 0.4 s fades): `rain, wind, city, roomtone, murmur, hum`. Use them on most shots for a continuous world, at −18 to −28.

FILE: skills/make-2d-video/references/story-breakdown.md

# Story breakdown (the creative pass)

Read the whole transcript twice. Then write `story/bible.md` (short, practical):
- **Logline and tone** (wonder / suspense / comedy / drama), plus who "you" is if the script is in second person.
- **Cast table.** For each recurring character: id, role, outfit `{top, color, head, headColor, face, legs, gloves, kid}`, signature colour, personality, and the expressions they use most. Every recurring character gets exactly ONE distinct saturated signature item (style-bible.md, Characters). Put the outfits in `scenes/cast.js`.
- **Locations (sets).** For each: name, mood (`day|golden|dusk|night|storm|interiorWarm|interiorCool|void`), where the key light comes from, the window/view, 3–6 signature props, and the layers (sky → far → mid → floor → foreground).
- **Motifs:** recurring visual devices tied to the story, such as a frozen-droplets look for slowed time, a corner multiplier badge, a heartbeat line, or a colour that marks a power.
- **Act / music plan:** sections with a mood and intensity (sound-design.md).

Then **rewrite `story/shots.json`** (schema in engine-api.md, story/shots.json). For every shot set:
`id, start, end, line, scene` (one concrete sentence describing the picture, e.g. "OTS: blurred Jake shoulder fg, Max at the window, rain, slow push"), `set`, `bars`, `in` (transition), `sfx`, `amb`, `notes`.

Pacing rules:
- Shots are **contiguous**: each `end` equals the next `start`. The first starts at 0 and the last ends at `duration`, which is the voiceover length plus about 1.5 s.
- Cut on phrase boundaries. Start a shot **0.1–0.2 s before** the phrase it illustrates.
- Target a **median of 2–3.5 s**. Fast action: 0.8–2 s cuts (split one phrase into several shots). Emotional or dialogue holds: 4–8 s. Never go beyond ~10 s without a new action or camera move.
- Every new location opens with an establishing shot (wide 2D or 3D).
- Expect roughly **15–25 shots per minute** of narration.

Give the user a 5–10 line summary: the cast, the locations, the number of shots, and the visual highlights per act. Continue unless they object.

# Style bible: the aiTrends 2D look

Follow this closely. It is the only description of the target look; no reference images are available.

## The look in one paragraph
Pure black (`S().ink`) stick figures, each with a circle head and thick round-capped line limbs, carry a thin rim-light edge on the side facing the light. They act inside **low-key, cinematic, flat-shaded vector environments**: one strong light source per scene (a sunset window, a street lamp, stadium floodlights, a monitor), deep purple or navy shadows, warm amber highlights, soft glows, floating dust or rain, shallow depth of field (blurred foreground and background layers), a slow constant camera drift, letterboxed frames, film grain and a vignette. Characters are recognised by ONE coloured garment or accessory. Faces are minimal: white dot or ring eyes, a simple white mouth shape, and occasional brows. The rhythm is broken up with giant face close-ups that fill the frame, freeze-frame moments with particles hanging in the air, glowing animated numbers and charts, navy blueprint diagrams for explanations, and low-poly 3D shots that use the same figures and palette. The overall feel is "a stick-figure film lit by a cinematographer": simple shapes, sophisticated light.

## Characters
- **Construction** (handled by `drawFigure`): head circle r = 26 rig units, torso 15, limbs 11–13 with round caps, short forward-pointing foot stubs, no fingers at normal sizes. Hands and fingers appear only in insert close-ups (thick capsules).
- **Depth shading:** the near arm and leg are pure ink. The far arm and leg use `inkFar`, a dark purple-grey. That's automatic.
- **Rim light on every lit character:** pass `rim: { color: M.rim, dir: [dx, dy] }`, where `dir` points from the figure toward the light. Backlit by a window or sun behind → `[0, -1]`. Light from the left → `[-1, -0.3]`. Width 4–5 at 1080p. The rim is what makes black-on-dark readable, so never skip it.
- **Signature items** (exactly one strong colour per recurring character; keep it identical all video): red-orange hoodie `#d64a33`, yellow cap `#f2c21a`, pink beanie `#f29ac0`, green cap `#3fb950`, blue tee `#3a6ea5`, purple tracksuit `#8a5ad6`, white lab coat + round glasses (scientist), black trench + fedora + sunglasses (mysterious mentor or antagonist, often half in shadow), headband in any accent colour (athlete), ponytail or bun to add variety. Children: `kid: true` (72% size). Background people and crowds: plain ink with no colour item.
- **Faces** are drawn on the side the figure faces (3/4 view). Expressions are the main acting tool. Change them **on the beat of the words**, not randomly:
  `neutral, smile, happy, joy, surprised, shocked, scared, worried, nervous, angry, determined, sad, cry, smirk, sleepy, thinking, confused, awe`.
  Blink every 2.5–4 s (`blink: (t % 3.1) > 3.0`, with a different period per character).
- **Talking:** only when a character speaks a quoted line. Use `talk: talkAmount(info.globalT, lineStart, lineEnd)` (driven by real word timings) and add 2–3 faint expanding `FX.rings` around the head. The narrator is never lip-synced.
- **Acting:** poses must read as silhouettes. Use `POSES` with `posesAt(t, [[0,'stand'],[0.6,'point']])`, blending ~0.3 s, and wrap standing or sitting figures in `breathe(pose, t, 1, seed)` so nobody is frozen. The exception is freeze-time moments, where the world is frozen and only the hero moves. Walking and running use `walkPose(phase, {run})`, moving x at a matching speed (walk ≈ 160 units/s × s, run ≈ 420 units/s × s, with phase = t × 2π × 0.9 for walk and × 1.6 for run). Use `trail: {count:4, dx:-40}` for super-speed afterimages.
- **Scale:** wide shots `s` ≈ 0.8–1.2 (figures fill 15–25% of the frame height). Medium shots `s` ≈ 1.5–1.8. Emotional close-ups use `bigFace` (radius 280–360 at 1080p, head slightly below centre, shoulders cropped by the frame bottom, with a rim crescent on the lit side). Always put figures on a floor with a contact shadow (automatic) and never let them float.

## Sets and environments
- Built from **flat shapes and soft linear gradients**: no outlines, no photos, no clip-art, no emoji. Texture is limited to floor lines, window mullions, blinds and lit-window grids.
- **Value structure:** 60–75% of the frame is dark mid-tones (walls and floors from the mood, usually darkened with `shade(c, -0.3)`), with 1–3 bright accents (a window, lamps, screens, floodlights, a sunset). **Stage black figures in front of the brightest area** (a window, a light pool, a glowing screen) so their silhouettes read.
- **Depth layers** with parallax via `layer(g, cam, depth, fn)`: sky 0.1–0.2 → skyline/hills 0.3–0.4 → back wall/facade 0.5–0.6 → floor/props 0.8–0.9 → characters 1.0 → blurred foreground 1.5–1.8 (`blurLayer(g, 8–14, fn, cam, 1.7)`). Most medium shots should have something soft in the foreground (glassware, plants, a railing, crowd heads, another character's shoulder).
- **Windows are the signature element.** Large windows with mullions and a sky gradient behind (sunset, golden, or night with a skyline of lit windows), a sun or moon glow, sometimes blinds. Night streets have house facades with warm lit windows, steps, lamp posts with light cones, parked cars and trees as round silhouettes.
- Typical locations to invent from the script: labs, cafés, bedrooms (moonlight on the bed), kitchens, classrooms, offices, hospitals, locker rooms, stadiums (tiered crowd rows, floodlight rigs, giant screen), streets, rooftops, piers at sunset, roads at night, parks, airports, space. Build each as a reusable `sets/<name>.js` function anchored to `GY` (the ground line: `E.H * V(0.8, 0.68)`) and `CX = E.W/2`. Extend walls, floors and skies far beyond the frame so camera moves never reveal edges.
- **Atmosphere in nearly every shot:** dust motes indoors (`FX.dust`), rain streaks for storms and night (`FX.rain`), frozen droplets or bubbles for time-stop (`FX.drops`), stars in night skies, a lens streak across bright sources (`FX.lensStreak`), god rays from suns and windows (`FX.godRays`), and soft fog bands.

## Light and colour
- Pick a **mood** per location: `M = mood('night')` gives `sky[], ambient, key, rim, wall, floor, dark, practical, grass, road, fog`. Take every colour from `M`, `S()` (style tokens) or the cast. Avoid hard-coded colours so style switching works.
- The story drives the mood: tense or scary beats go darker and cooler (night/storm/interiorCool), triumphant or tender beats go warm (golden/dusk/interiorWarm), and explanations use `void`/blueprint.
- **One key light per shot.** Show it (a sun, lamp or window) and spill it: `glow()` around the source, `FX.pool()` on the floor under it, `FX.lightCone()` from lamps, `FX.godRays()` from windows and suns.
- The global colour grade comes from the chosen style and is applied automatically to every final frame, 2D and 3D alike: per-channel lift/gamma/gain (lift tints shadows, gain tints highlights), contrast, saturation, vignette and grain. Bloom applies to 3D. This is why scenes must use mood and style colours: the grade then makes each style look distinct.

## Camera (never fully static)
Every shot moves: a slow push-in (`z` 1.0 → 1.05–1.10 over the shot), a lateral drift (`x` ±20–60), a slow pull-out for reveals, a slight roll (`r` ±0.02) for unease, or shake (`shake(t, amp)`) for impacts. Use `ease.inOut` for moves and `ease.out` for snaps.

**Shot vocabulary** (use all of these across a video, roughly in these proportions):
1. **Establishing wide** (10%): the location with small figures and a slow push. Opens each new scene. Often 3D.
2. **Medium two-shot** (25%): side view, characters facing each other, conversation and action.
3. **Over-the-shoulder** (10%): a big blurred silhouette of one character in the foreground (`blurLayer`, depth 1.6), the other sharp.
4. **Giant face close-up** (`bigFace`, 10–15%): reactions, realisations, "you" moments, dialogue lines. Background = the location, blurred and darkened (`blurLayer` + `veil`).
5. **Insert / macro** (10%): hands, a phone screen, a watch, a ball, a cup, a door handle. Big, simple, blurred background.
6. **Top-down / diagram view** (5%): e.g. a table from above with a dotted trajectory and a timing label.
7. **Freeze frame / time-stop** (signature moment, 1–3 per video): particles hanging in the air, a cool tint, a corner badge (`100x`), slow rings, a slow rotation, only the hero moving. Enter with a `flash` transition and a `freeze` SFX.
8. **3D orbit / fly-through** (about 1 per minute): big spaces like stadiums, streets and halls, using `three-kit` (engine-api.md, three-kit).
9. **Data card / UI** (as needed): any number the narrator says appears on screen and animates as it's spoken.
10. **Blueprint diagram**: the explanatory or science segment (navy grid, light-blue line art, gold highlights, labels synced to words).
11. **Title card**: glowing caps title over a cinematic wide (often after the cold-open hook).
12. **End screen**: the question in serif italic, a comment bubble, a subscribe pill, and the whole cast lined up.

**Letterbox (`bars`):** `wide` (2:1) is the default. `scope` (2.39:1) is for drama: the title, freezes, climaxes and intense close-ups. Use `none` for full-frame UI or blueprint if wanted. Bars animate smoothly between shots.

## Editing and transitions
Hard **cut** by default (80% or more). **flash** (white) into freeze, time-stop or superpower moments and big realisations. **dip** (to black) for time skips and chapter changes. **fade** for gentle or dreamy moments and the title card. **whip** for energetic jumps between places. **zoom** to dive into an explanation. Keep continuity: the same outfits, the same screen direction (in a conversation, the hero is on the left facing right), and the same set geometry for every shot in a scene.

## On-screen graphics and typography
- **Fonts:** Fredoka Bold for titles and big lines (ALL CAPS, letter-spaced, warm glow). Nunito Black for labels and numbers. Playfair Display Black Italic for the end-screen question.
- **Colours:** gold `S().ui.gold` for key numbers, coral `ui.accent` for alarming values, cream `ui.cream` for labels, blueprint blue `ui.blueprint`.
- **Toolkit (`ui2d`):** `titleCard`, `bigLine` (a question or chapter line), `badge` (corner multiplier or day counter), `readout` ("NORMAL: 0.25 s"), `dataCard` (title, counter and a curve drawing on), `compareBars`, `dimension` (arrowed measure "30 m"), `callout` (leader-line label), `meter`, `blueprint` + `lineFigure`, `bubble`, `endScreen`, `captions`.
- Every number or measurement the narrator says should appear, synced with `wordTime()`. Keep text minimal: at most 1–2 short labels at once, and never sentences except the title, the question and the end screen.
- Pop graphics in with `ease.outBack` scale and a `pop`/`blip` SFX. Fade them out before the cut.

## Story conventions ("What if…?" narratives; use only what the script contains)
Cold-open hook (a dramatic moment, often a freeze) → title card → normal life and the discovery → escalation with concrete numbers → the cost or twist → the climax → the resolution → "Is any of this real?" (blueprint science segment) → end screen with a question and CTA. Explainer, history and documentary scripts use the same vocabulary: characters act out examples, data cards carry numbers, blueprint diagrams explain mechanisms.

## Don't
No realism, gradients on characters, thick outlines, photos, emoji, clip-art or flat white backgrounds (except flashes). No static shots. No figures without rim light in lit scenes. No narrator lip-sync. No walls of text. No more than ~4 characters in focus at once (crowds are fine). No floating figures. No outfit colour changes. No centring everything (use thirds; the face close-up is the exception). No hard-coded colours that ignore `mood()`/`S()`. No `Math.random()` or `Date.now()` in scenes (they break determinism; use `rng(seed)` and `t`).


## 9:16 vertical rules
The logical stage is 1080×1920 (`E.vertical === true`). Use `V(landscape, vertical)` for every size and position that depends on the aspect.
- **Closer framing:** medium shots and close-ups dominate. Wides become tall stacks (sky → building → ground), with figures in the middle third.
- **Safe area:** keep faces and text inside the central ~900×1500, away from platform UI. Captions sit at ~79% of the height (`captions: true` in config.json); auto-shrink long phrases.
- Diagrams stack vertically (eye above brain above arm). Data cards are taller. Font sizes are larger (the UI helpers already use `V()`).
- Bars default to `none`, and `scope` gives thin bars. Ground line `GY = E.H * 0.68`, which keeps characters above the captions (y ≈ 0.79·H). Set `"captions": false` on title-card or end-screen shots so the words don't duplicate on-screen text.
- Check with `--sheet` in 9:16 before rendering. Shots designed only for 16:9 usually crop the subject.

  "create an animated explainer from my audio", or "do the whole video". It runs the full pipeline:
  install, timestamps (Whisper), story breakdown, style choice, scene building, rendering, music, SFX and
  final MP4 in 16:9 or 9:16 at 720p to 4K.

# aiTrends 2D Studio: full video pipeline

Turn a voiceover (plus an optional script) into a finished MP4 where every frame is drawn by code (HTML canvas + Three.js), captured frame by frame in headless Chromium and encoded with ffmpeg. No image or video generation models are used, so characters stay consistent and text stays crisp. Invent the visuals fresh for every script; only the style is fixed.

**Bundled resources (this skill's base directory):**
- `kit/`: the tested studio (renderer, engine, tools, audio, the `examples/reaction-time` reference project). Install it into the workspace as `aitrends-2d-studio/` (see `references/install.md`).
- `references/install.md`: install, dependency table, doctor, self-test.
- `references/style-bible.md`: **the look** (characters, sets, light, camera, editing, graphics, 9:16 rules, don'ts). Read it fully before designing any shot.
- `references/story-breakdown.md`: writing `story/bible.md` and `story/shots.json`, plus pacing rules.
- `references/engine-api.md`: the scene API, config and shots.json schemas, and the three-kit reference.
- `references/sound-design.md`: the music plan, SFX library, ambience and levels.
- `references/render-qc.md`: preview and final render, speed guide, QC checklist, troubleshooting.

Stage commands from this plugin re-run one stage: `/new-video` (stages 1–4), `/video-styles` (5), `/render-video` (6 and 8), `/video-sound` (7). When running end to end, follow this file and load references as each stage needs them.

## Golden rules
1. **Keep the narration intact.** Never rewrite, add, drop or reorder spoken words. Every shot illustrates the words spoken while it is on screen.
2. **Same style, new pictures.** Invent sets, props, characters and actions from this script. Use `examples/reaction-time` only to learn the API.
3. **Show what is said, literally and specifically.** Abstract lines become visual metaphors, diagrams or close-up reactions.
4. **Draft, look, fix, then render.** Open contact sheets and stills yourself (read the PNGs) before any full render. Rendering is slow; looking is fast.
5. **Three user checkpoints:** setup choices, style choice from rendered samples, and the preview before the final render. If the user is away, take the defaults, state what was assumed, and continue.
6. **Files are the truth.** Each video lives in `aitrends-2d-studio/projects/<slug>/`, and `story/shots.json` owns all timing.

## Stage 1: Intake
Ask once (AskUserQuestion when available) for:
- **Voiceover file** (required).
- **Script:** timestamped (SRT/VTT/JSON/TXT with `[mm:ss]`), plain text (best: aligned to the audio, keeping exact wording), or none (transcribed).
- **Aspect:** 16:9 or 9:16.
- **Resolution:** 720p / 1080p (recommended) / 1440p / 2160p (4K). Mention that higher resolutions take much longer to render.
- **Captions:** default on for 9:16, off for 16:9.
- **Music:** generated, the user's own file, or none. **SFX:** on or off.
- **Title.**

Don't ask about style yet. Defaults: 16:9, 1080p, 24 fps, generated music + SFX, style `dusk`.

## Stage 2: Install or check the studio
Follow `references/install.md`: copy `kit/` to `aitrends-2d-studio/` in the workspace (skip if already present), install dependencies, run `python tools/verify_kit.py` and `python tools/doctor.py`, then run the example self-test. Report what was installed in one short list.

## Stage 3: Project + timeline
Run inside `aitrends-2d-studio/`. Use `python3` where `python` isn't Python 3.
```bash
python tools/new_project.py --name <slug> --voice "<voiceover path>" [--script "<script path>"] --aspect 16:9 --resolution 1080p --title "<title>" [--captions]
```
- Timestamped script: `python tools/parse_script.py --project projects/<slug> --script projects/<slug>/input/script.<ext>`
- Plain script: `python tools/transcribe.py --project projects/<slug> --script projects/<slug>/input/script.txt --lang en --model small`. The match must be ≥ 90%; if it's lower, tell the user the script and audio differ.
- No script: `python tools/transcribe.py --project projects/<slug> --lang en --model small` (`base` on slow machines, `large-v3` for hard audio).

Check `timeline/transcript.txt` (its end should be near the voiceover length), then run `python tools/beats.py --project projects/<slug>` to get the beat sheet and a draft `story/shots.json`.

## Stage 4: Story breakdown
Read `references/style-bible.md` and `references/story-breakdown.md`. Write `story/bible.md` (logline, cast with one signature colour each, locations with moods and key lights, motifs, music plan). Write `scenes/cast.js`. Rewrite `story/shots.json`: contiguous shots, cut on phrase boundaries 0.1–0.2 s early, a median of 2–3.5 s, an establishing shot per new location, and a concrete `scene` description for each. Send the user a 5–10 line plan summary and continue unless they object.

## Stage 5: Style choice
Build the 1–2 most-used sets plus 3 representative shots (a wide shot with ≥ 2 characters, a `bigFace` close-up, a data/blueprint or 3D shot). Then render:
```bash
node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --styles dusk,midnight,golden,neon,storybook --at <t1>,<t2>,<t3> --scale 0.5
```
Open `build/styles/style_grid.png`, then show it with one line per style: **dusk** (mauve shadows, amber key, cream rim), **midnight** (cold navy, blue-white rims, sodium practicals), **golden** (warm orange and teal), **neon** (deep blacks, magenta and teal), **storybook** (soft pastel, low contrast). Save the pick in `config.json` → `style`. For tweaks, add a custom entry to `STYLES` in `engine/styles.js` (moods overrides plus the `grade` lift/gamma/gain, sat, contrast, vignette, grain, bloom) and re-render that row.

## Stage 6: Build every shot
Read `references/engine-api.md` and the example scenes in `examples/reaction-time/scenes/` once. Work in batches of 8–12 shots:
1. Write `scenes/sets/<location>.js` (reusable, anchored to `GY = E.H * V(0.8, 0.68)` and `CX = E.W/2`, with walls, floor and sky extended past the frame) and `scenes/shots/NN_<name>.js` with `registerShot('<id>', {...})` for the ids in shots.json.
2. Run `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --sheet --scale 0.5`. Open every `build/sheets/sheet_XX.png` and check it against the QC checklist in `references/render-qc.md`. Fix any MISSING shots, errors printed by the renderer, and composition problems.
3. Check motion with `--stills t1,t2,t3` or `--from A --to B --scale 0.5` (writes `build/preview.mp4`).

Non-negotiables from the style bible:
- Rim light on every lit figure.
- Colours from `mood()`/`S()`.
- A camera move in every shot.
- Atmosphere (dust, rain, glows).
- Moody value structure.
- Graphics synced to words with `wordTime()`.
- `V()` for every aspect-dependent value.
- No `Math.random`/`Date.now`.

## Stage 7: Sound
Read `references/sound-design.md`. Fill `story/shots.json` → `music` (contiguous mood sections) and add per-shot `sfx` cues and `amb` beds. Mixing happens after the final render (Stage 8), or run `python audio/mix.py --project projects/<slug>` now to audition `build/mix.wav`.

## Stage 8: Render and deliver
Read `references/render-qc.md`.
1. Preview: `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --from 0 --to 30 --scale 0.5`. Show the user the preview or a contact sheet before the long render.
2. Final: `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug>`. Run it in the background for long videos and poll the log. The log prints fps and ETA, so share the estimate.
3. Run `python audio/mix.py --project projects/<slug>`, which generates music and SFX, ducks the music under the voice, normalises to −14 LUFS and muxes `out/<title>.mp4`.
4. QC the final file: duration matches, audio is present, and a 1-frame-per-10-s contact sheet looks right. Deliver the MP4 (send the file), then give the location in plain words and one line on what to tweak next if needed.

## Defaults and judgement calls
- When unsure about a visual, choose the more literal, more cinematic option: a close-up for emotion, an insert for objects, a blueprint for mechanisms, 3D for big spaces.
- Keep about 15–25 shots per minute. Use 1–3 freeze-frames per video and about one 3D shot per minute.
- Keep each character's outfit colour fixed for the whole video, and keep screen direction consistent within a scene.
- Prefer re-rendering a fixed shot range over the whole video only when the user is comfortable splicing. Otherwise re-render everything.

# /new-video: intake → install → timeline → story breakdown

This command covers stages 1–4 of the aiTrends 2D Studio pipeline. Shared references live in the sibling skill folder `../make-2d-video/references/` (relative to this skill's base directory), and the studio kit lives in `../make-2d-video/kit/`.

## 1. Intake
Ask once (AskUserQuestion when available), skipping anything the user already said:
- **Voiceover file** (required).
- **Script:** timestamped (SRT, VTT, JSON, or TXT with `[mm:ss]`), plain text, or none.
- **Aspect:** 16:9 or 9:16.
- **Resolution:** 720p / 1080p / 1440p / 2160p.
- **Captions:** default on for 9:16.
- **Music:** generated, own file, or none. **SFX:** on or off.
- **Title.**

Defaults: 16:9, 1080p, generated music + SFX.

## 2. Studio
If `aitrends-2d-studio/render.mjs` doesn't exist in the workspace, follow `../make-2d-video/references/install.md` in full. Otherwise run `python tools/doctor.py` inside it and fix anything reported.

## 3. Project and timestamps (inside `aitrends-2d-studio/`)
```bash
python tools/new_project.py --name <slug> --voice "<voiceover>" [--script "<script>"] --aspect <16:9|9:16> --resolution <res> --title "<title>" [--captions]
```
- **Timestamped script** → `python tools/parse_script.py --project projects/<slug> --script projects/<slug>/input/script.<ext>`. It accepts SRT, VTT, JSON (`[{start,end,text}]`, `{segments}`, `{words}`) and TXT lines like `[00:12.5] text`, `00:12 - 00:15 text` or `00:00:12,500 --> 00:00:15,000 text`. If the parser reports no timestamps, fall back to the plain-script route.
- **Plain script** → `python tools/transcribe.py --project projects/<slug> --script projects/<slug>/input/script.txt --lang <code> --model small`. It keeps the script's exact words with Whisper timing. Require ≥ 90% match; if lower, tell the user the script and audio differ.
- **No script** → `python tools/transcribe.py --project projects/<slug> --lang <code> --model small` (`base` for speed, `large-v3` for difficult audio). The first run downloads the model.

Open `timeline/transcript.txt` and confirm it reads correctly and ends near the voiceover length. Then run `python tools/beats.py --project projects/<slug>` (add `--force` to overwrite an existing shots.json).

## 4. Story breakdown
Read `../make-2d-video/references/style-bible.md` and `../make-2d-video/references/story-breakdown.md` fully, then:
1. Write `projects/<slug>/story/bible.md`: logline and tone, the cast (one saturated signature item each), locations (mood, key light, window view, props), motifs and the music plan.
2. Write `projects/<slug>/scenes/cast.js` with the outfits.
3. Rewrite `projects/<slug>/story/shots.json` following the pacing rules. Every shot needs a concrete `scene` description, plus transitions, bars, sfx and amb where they matter.
4. Send the user a 5–10 line summary: the cast, the locations, the shot count, and the highlights per act.

Finish by saying the next step is `/video-styles` (or continue with the full pipeline in the `make-2d-video` skill if the user asked for the whole video).

# /render-video: build, QC, preview and final render

Work inside `aitrends-2d-studio/` on the active project (`projects/<slug>`; if several exist, ask which one). Shared references are in `../make-2d-video/references/` relative to this skill's base directory. Read `engine-api.md`, `style-bible.md` and `render-qc.md` before writing or fixing scenes.

## 1. Build or repair shots (batches of 8–12)
- List what's missing: `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --sheet --scale 0.4`. The console lists MISSING shots and shot errors, and `build/sheets/sheet_XX.png` shows one still per shot.
- Write `scenes/sets/*.js` and `scenes/shots/NN_name.js` (`registerShot(id, …)` for each shot id). Study `examples/reaction-time/scenes/` for working patterns.
- After each batch, re-run the sheet and **open every sheet PNG** against the QC checklist in `render-qc.md`. Fix and repeat until there are no MISSING or ERROR frames and every shot passes.
- To check motion, use `--stills t1,t2,t3` (several moments of one shot) or `--from A --to B --scale 0.5` (writes `build/preview.mp4`).

## 2. Preview checkpoint
Render `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --from 0 --to 30 --scale 0.5` and send the user the preview, or the contact sheets, before the long render. Mention the expected render time (speed guide in `render-qc.md`: frames = seconds × 24; ~2 frames/s at 720p on a small 2-core CPU, 1080p ~2× slower, 4K ~8× slower, desktops 3–5× faster).

## 3. Final render
- To change resolution or aspect, edit `projects/<slug>/config.json` (`resolution`, `aspect`). Switching aspect needs a fresh sheet review, because 9:16 framing differs.
- Run `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> [--workers N] [--format jpeg]` and run it in the background for anything over a minute or two: `nohup node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> > build/render.log 2>&1 &`. Poll the log and report progress and ETA at sensible intervals.
- Use fewer workers if memory is tight (about 1–1.5 GB each at 1080p, more at 4K). With no GPU, add `--gl swiftshader` (already the Linux default).
- Output: `build/video_silent.mp4`. The end of the log lists any shot errors. Fix them and re-render if there are any.

## 4. Sound, mux and deliver
Make sure the music plan and SFX are set (see `/video-sound`), then run `python audio/mix.py --project projects/<slug>`, which writes `out/<title>.mp4`. QC: `ffprobe` duration ≈ `story/shots.json` duration, an audio stream is present, and a contact sheet of the final file (`ffmpeg -i out/<file>.mp4 -vf fps=1/10,scale=320:-1,tile=6x5 -frames:v 1 build/final_sheet.png`) looks right. Send the MP4 to the user and say in plain words where it was saved.
