  or "redo the soundtrack" for an aiTrends 2D Studio video. It plans mood-based generated music, places SFX and
  ambience cues on the shot list, mixes them under the voiceover with ducking and loudness normalisation, and muxes

# /video-sound: music, SFX, ambience and the final mix

Work inside `aitrends-2d-studio/` on the active project. Read `../make-2d-video/references/sound-design.md` (relative to this skill's base directory) first.

## 1. Music plan
- Read `story/bible.md` and the shot list, then write `story/shots.json` → `"music"`: contiguous sections `{start, end, mood, intensity}` that change at story turns (title card, discovery, danger, climax, resolution, explanation), typically every 30–90 s.
- Moods: `mystery, tension, action, wonder, hopeful, sad, triumph, playful, calm, dark, epic`. Intensity 0–1 adds pulse, arpeggios and drums.
- Audition a mood: `python audio/music.py --mood tension --intensity 0.7 --dur 20 --out build/test_tension.wav`, and send the WAV if the user wants to listen.
- **User's own track:** copy it into `projects/<slug>/input/` and set `config.json` → `"music": {"enabled": true, "file": "input/<name>.mp3", "gainDb": -17}`. It loops or trims, and the end fades.
- **No music:** `"music": {"enabled": false}`.

## 2. SFX and ambience
- Per shot: `"sfx": [{"at": <s from shot start>, "name": "<effect>", "gain": -8}]` (optional `dur`, `seed`, `pitch`; `name` may be a path to the user's own WAV/MP3 inside the project). Per shot ambience: `"amb": {"name": "rain|wind|city|roomtone|murmur|hum", "gain": -20}`.
- List the library with `python audio/sfx.py --list`, or audition it with `python audio/sfx.py --demo build/sfx_demo`.
- Place cues on actions and graphics: `whoosh`/`swish` on whips and fast moves, `pop`/`blip` when graphics appear, `counter` under ticking numbers, `riser` then `impact`/`stinger` on reveals, `slowmo`/`freeze` for time effects, `hit`/`kick`/`footstep` on contact. Never stack more than ~2 cues at the same instant.

## 3. Mix and mux
Run `python audio/mix.py --project projects/<slug>`. It ducks the music −9 dB and ambience −5 dB under the voice, normalises to `config.loudness` (−14 LUFS default), writes `build/mix.wav`, and, when `build/video_silent.mp4` exists, writes `out/<title>.mp4`.
- Levels: `config.music.gainDb` (default −17 relative to the voice) and `config.sfx.gainDb` (default −6). Change them by 2–3 dB per iteration.
- QC: listen points or `ffmpeg -i out/<file>.mp4 -af ebur128=framelog=quiet -f null -` (integrated loudness about −14 LUFS) and check that the duration matches the video.

name: video-styles

  "pick a visual style", "make it warmer / darker / more neon", "create a custom style", or "change the colour grade"
  for an aiTrends 2D Studio video. It renders the same shots of the user's story in five preset styles
  (dusk, midnight, golden, neon, storybook) as a comparison grid, records the choice, and builds custom styles.
metadata:
  version: "0.1.0"
  author: aiTrends

# /video-styles: render style options and lock the look

Work inside `aitrends-2d-studio/` on the active project (`projects/<slug>`; if several exist, ask which one). Shared references are in `../make-2d-video/references/` relative to this skill's base directory.

## 1. Make sure there is something representative to show
The grid uses the user's own shots, never the bundled example. If the project has fewer than 3 finished shots, first build (following `../make-2d-video/references/style-bible.md` and `engine-api.md`):
- `scenes/cast.js` and the 1–2 most-used sets,
- a wide set shot with ≥ 2 characters, a `bigFace` close-up, and a data/blueprint or 3D shot.

Pick one time inside each shot (about 45% through it) for the grid columns.

## 2. Render the grid
```bash
node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --styles dusk,midnight,golden,neon,storybook --at <t1>,<t2>,<t3> --scale 0.5
```
Open `build/styles/style_grid.png` (one row per style) and check it, then send it to the user with one line each:
- **dusk — Cinematic Dusk:** mauve-purple shadows, amber key light, cream rim light (the reference look).
- **midnight — Midnight Blue:** cold navy, blue-white rims, sodium-orange practicals; good for night, rain, thrillers.
- **golden — Golden Hour:** warm orange-and-teal, big sun glows; uplifting and nostalgic.
- **neon — Neon Noir:** deep blacks, magenta and teal rims, strong bloom; sci-fi, tech, crime.
- **storybook — Soft Storybook:** lighter pastel, low contrast; family-friendly.

Ask which they want (AskUserQuestion when available).

## 3. Save or customise
- Preset chosen → set `"style"` in `projects/<slug>/config.json`.
- Tweaks requested → add a new entry to `STYLES` in `engine/styles.js`. Copy the closest preset, give it a new id and a `name`/`desc`, then adjust:
  - `moods` overrides (per mood: `sky`, `key`, `rim`, `wall`, `floor`, `practical`),
  - `grade`: `lift` [r,g,b] tints the shadows (raise b for cooler shadows), `gain` [r,g,b] tints the highlights (raise r for warmer), `gamma` [r,g,b], `sat`, `contrast`, `vignette`, `grain`, `bloom` (3D),
  - `ui` colours (gold, accent, cream, blueprint) and `rimWidth`.

  Re-render with `--styles <newid>,<closest preset> --at …` so the user can compare, iterate until they're happy, then set it in config.json. `tools/verify_kit.py` will now report `engine/styles.js` as changed; that's expected.

Scenes only follow a style switch if they take colours from `mood()`/`S()`. If one shot ignores the style, find the hard-coded colours in it and replace them.
